  <div  class="popup-content">
    
<form class="search-form" role="search" method="get" id="" action="https://www.group-ib.com/">
  <input type="search" placeholder="Search" name="s" id="search-input" value=""/>
  <button type="submit" class="search-button"><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/search-icon.svg" alt="Search Icon"></button>

<style>
  .hidden {
    display: none;
  }
</style>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
  const openSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.open-search-popup')];
  const closeSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.close-search-popup')];
  const searchPopup = document.querySelector('.search-popup');

  const closePopup = () => {
    searchPopup.style.display = 'none';
    openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.add('hidden'));
  };

  openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(openSearchPopupBtn => {
    openSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();

      searchPopup.style.display = 'block';
      openSearchPopupBtn.classList.add('hidden');
      closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    });
  });

  closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(closeSearchPopupBtn => {
    closeSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      closePopup();
    });
  });

  document.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target) && !openSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target)) && !closeSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target))) {
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target)) {
          closePopup();
        }
      }, 100);
    }
  });
</script>    </div>

                            <div class="share-url"><input type="text" id="share-url-mobile"/><div class="share-url__copy"></div></div></div></div><div class="post-info__toggle  share-toggle"><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/share-black.svg" alt=""></div><div class="post-info__toggle theme-toggle"><img class="theme-toggle--sun" src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/wb_sunny-black.svg" alt=""><img class="theme-toggle--moon" src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/moon.svg" alt=""></div></div></div>
                            <h1 class="header__title title-h2 mb-24 mt-md-48 mt-lg-64 mt-xl-64">Kremlin RATs from Nigeria</h1><p class="header__text mb-xs-48 mb-64 mb-lg-96 mb-xl-96">The analysis of phishing campaigns carried out by a new threat actor</p><div class="post-info__date mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><div class="body2">April 5, 2021 · <span id="reading-time"></span> min to read · Threat Intelligence</div></div><div class="post-info__image mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-25.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img data-no-lazy="" fetchpriority="high" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-25.jpg" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed"></picture></div><div class="post-info__tags"><div class="post-info__tag body2">Nigeria</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Phishing</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">RATs</div></div>                        </div>
                    </div>
                </div>
                <div class="blog-post-content" id="blog-post-content">
                    <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>In May 2020, Group-IB&#8217;s network graph — an automated tool for analyzing adversary infrastructure — first detected domains with an unusual pattern (<em>*kremlin*.duckdns.org</em>) to which various malicious files were connecting. Group-IB&#8217;s <a href="/products/threat-intelligence/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Threat Intelligence</a> team <strong>examined the domains and identified three phishing campaigns that were used to deliver various RATs, such as NetWire or AsyncRAT.</strong> The campaigns had been active since 2019 and targeted users in Poland, Turkey, Italy, Spain, Ukraine, Russia, Kazakhstan, Bulgaria, Belarus, Greece, and the Czech Republic.</p>
<p>Analysis of these campaigns revealed that <strong>the threat actor used phishing emails with malicious attachments to gain initial access</strong>. Office documents exploiting vulnerabilities in Microsoft Office products, as well as malicious macros were used as attachments. Group-IB discovered more than 100 phishing email samples distributed en masse.</p>
<p>Depending on the geographical distribution of the targets the cybercriminals altered the email contents and language. <strong>Phishing emails were accompanied by fake purchasing orders and other financial documents</strong>, used COVID-19 as a theme sometimes and were made to look like legitimate communications from banks or well-known logistics companies.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-24"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-8.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-8.jpg" alt="Kremlin RATs Phishing email" title="1-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text"></p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-3.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-3.jpg" alt="Kremlin RATs Phishing email" title="2-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text"></p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>
                 
                  <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">

                      grabCursor: true,

                        nextEl: '.swiper-button-next',
                        prevEl: '.swiper-button-prev',
                      },

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The unconventional naming pattern for the domains used to deliver malware (<em>*kremlin, *crimea, *putin</em>) might be an amateurish attempt to imitate Russian speaking cybercriminals to throw researchers off track and complicate attribution.</p>
<p>Nevertheless, during further analysis of the adversary infrastructure and the TTPs employed, as well as a set of malicious software used in conjunction with DDNS services Group-IB researchers attributed the campaigns with high confidence to a previously unknown <strong>threat actor from Nigeria</strong>.</p>
<p>The post examines three threat actor&#8217;s campaigns carried out in 2019 and 2020, analyzes the adversary&#8217;s infrastructure and TTPs and provides recommendations and mitigation techniques to thwart the threat.</p>
<h2>Summer 2020 campaign</h2>
<p>The initial list of domains that caught our attention was:</p>
<ul>
<li>crimea-kremlin[.]duckdns.org</li>
<li>kremlin-afghan[.]duckdns.org</li>
<li>kremlin-crimea[.]duckdns.org</li>
<li>kremlin-turbo[.]duckdns.org</li>
</ul>
<p>These domains were registered to the same IP address (79.134.225[.]43) on June 15, 2020. According to the Group-IB network graph, there are about 30 different malicious files associated with these four domains. The decoys found suggest that the campaign targeted users in Poland, Turkey, Italy, Germany, and Bulgaria.</p>
<div id="attachment_26132" style="width: 1917px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26132" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26132 size-full" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201907%201080'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="kremlin RATs malicious infrastructure" width="1907" height="1080" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-24.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26132" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26132 size-full" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-24.png" alt="kremlin RATs malicious infrastructure" width="1907" height="1080" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26132" class="wp-caption-text">Malicious infrastructure identified by Group-IB&#8217;s graph</p></div>
<p>Further analysis showed that most of the files had been uploaded to public sources from June 25, 2020. The most common names were &#8220;<em>Potwierdzenie transakcji.xls</em>&#8220;, &#8220;<em>İşlem makbuzu</em>&#8220;, &#8220;<em>WACKER &#8211; 000160847.xls</em>&#8220;, and &#8220;<em>Potwierdzenie operacji.xls</em>&#8220;. One of these files (<em>SHA1: 95A6A416F682A9D254E76EC38ADE01CE241B3366</em>) is a decoy document in Polish supposedly sent from one of the local banks.</p>
<div id="attachment_26133" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26133" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26133" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20636'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Kremlin RATs decoy document" width="904" height="636" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-24.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26133" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26133" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-24.png" alt="Kremlin RATs decoy document" width="904" height="636" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26133" class="wp-caption-text">Decoy document SHA1: 95A6A416F682A9D254E76EC38ADE01CE241B3366</p></div>
<h3>Infection</h3>
<p>After the macros in this document are enabled, a PowerShell script is executed to extract the second-stage command from the <em>lab.jpg</em> file located on a remote server:</p>
<div id="attachment_26134" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26134" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26134" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%2040'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Executable Kremlin RATs PS script from a macro" width="904" height="40" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-26.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26134" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26134" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-26.png" alt="Executable Kremlin RATs PS script from a macro" width="904" height="40" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26134" class="wp-caption-text">Executable PS script from a macro</p></div>
<p>The <em>lab.jpg</em> file contains a Base64-encoded command that, after being decoded, looks as follows:</p>
<p><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26135" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20924%20270'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Deobfuscated contents of Kremlin RATs lab.jpg" width="924" height="270" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-19.png" /><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26135" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-19.png" alt="Deobfuscated contents of Kremlin RATs lab.jpg" width="924" height="270" /></noscript></p>
<p>This code reads the contents of the file <em>http://officeservicecorp[.]biz/rnp.txt</em>, which contains a payload.</p>
<p>As a result of this sequence of PowerShell scripts, the infamous NetWire RAT is loaded and executed. The tool connects to its C&amp;C server (<em>kremlin-crimea[.]duckdns.org</em>) on port 3396.</p>
<div id="attachment_26136" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26136" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26136" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20468'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="NetWire RAT configuration" width="904" height="468" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-20.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26136" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26136" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-20.png" alt="NetWire RAT configuration" width="904" height="468" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26136" class="wp-caption-text">NetWire RAT configuration</p></div>
<p>If we insert the original domains into the graph in increments of 2, we will see not only these domains, but also the rest of the associated infrastructure that was involved in all the infection stages.</p>
<div id="attachment_26137" style="width: 912px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26137" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26137" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20902%20662'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="kremlin RATs associated infrastructure" width="902" height="662" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-19.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26137" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26137" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-19.png" alt="kremlin RATs associated infrastructure" width="902" height="662" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26137" class="wp-caption-text">Graph in increments of 2. Associated infrastructure detected by Group-IB&#8217;s network graph</p></div>
<p>It is interesting to note that files that connected <em>office-service-tech[.]info</em> also performed network connections to ahjuric[.]si. One of these files (<em>SHA1 a3816c37d0fbe26a87d1cc7beff91ce5816039e7</em>) was a decoy document in Turkish with the logo of a state-owned bank in Turkey.</p>
<div id="attachment_26138" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26138" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26138" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20628'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="A kremlin RATs decoy document targeting Turkish users" width="904" height="628" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-15.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26138" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26138" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-15.png" alt="A kremlin RATs decoy document targeting Turkish users" width="904" height="628" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26138" class="wp-caption-text">A decoy document targeting Turkish users. SHA1: a3816c37d0fbe26a87d1cc7beff91ce5816039e7</p></div>
<p>This document contains a malicious macro that executes a PowerShell script. The latter reads Code.txt from a remote server and runs a chain of obfuscated PS scripts.</p>
<p><div id="attachment_26139" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26139" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26139" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%2026'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Contents of ahjuric[.]si/code.txt" width="904" height="26" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-17.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26139" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26139" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-17.png" alt="Contents of ahjuric[.]si/code.txt" width="904" height="26" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26139" class="wp-caption-text">Contents of ahjuric[.]si/code.txt</p></div><div id="attachment_26140" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26140" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26140" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20136'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Contents of ahjuric[.]si/code.txt" width="904" height="136" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-14.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26140" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26140" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-14.png" alt="Contents of ahjuric[.]si/code.txt" width="904" height="136" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26140" class="wp-caption-text">Contents of ahjuric[.]si/code.txt</p></div>Executing the obfuscated PS script leads to another Base64-encoded script being executed. The latter will ultimately execute the payload in the form of NetWire RAT from <em>office-service-tech[.]info/pld.txt</em>.</p>
<p><div id="attachment_26141" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26141" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26141" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20202'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Contents of office-service-tech[.]info/pld.txt" width="904" height="202" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min-13.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26141" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26141" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min-13.png" alt="Contents of office-service-tech[.]info/pld.txt" width="904" height="202" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26141" class="wp-caption-text">Contents of office-service-tech[.]info/pld.txt</p></div>The C&amp;C server of this sample is <em>crimea-kremlin.duckdns[.]org</em>.</p>
<p>We also found files that make network connections simultaneously to <em>kremlin-turbo.duckdns[.]org</em> and <em>wshsoft[.]company</em>. The latter domain name brings to mind the WSH RAT, which is based on the Houdini code. One of these files (<em>SHA1: b42a3b8c6d53a28a2dc84042d95ce9ca6e09cbcf</em>) is a RAT sample that sends requests such as /is-ready to the C&amp;C server <em>kremlin-turbo.duckdns[.]org:3397</em>. WSHRAT is indicated as its UA.</p>
<div id="attachment_26142" style="width: 814px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26142" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26142" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20804%201060'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Network requests of kremlin RATs file" width="804" height="1060" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-11.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26142" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26142" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-11.png" alt="Network requests of kremlin RATs file" width="804" height="1060" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26142" class="wp-caption-text">Network requests of the file with SHA1: b42a3b8c6d53a28a2dc84042d95ce9ca6e09cbcf</p></div>
<p>At this stage, it is important to note that some of the domains used in this campaign were registered to the email address <em>tetragulf@yahoo[.]com</em>.</p>
<p><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26143" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20814'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="kremlin RATs campaign domains names" width="904" height="814" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-2.webp" /><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26143" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-2.webp" alt="kremlin RATs campaign domains names" width="904" height="814" /></noscript></p>
<h2>Spring 2020 campaign</h2>
<p>While examining all other related infrastructure, we came across domains registered to <em>asetonly@yahoo[.]com</em>. Since early 2020, the following domains have been registered to this email address:</p>
<ol>
<li>nitro-malwrhunterteams[.]com</li>
<li>office-data-labs[.]com</li>
<li>putin-malwrhunterteams[.]com</li>
<li>kremlin-malwrhunterteam[.]info</li>
<li>skidware-malwrhunterteams[.]com</li>
<li>screw-malwrhunterteams[.]com</li>
<li>screw-malwrhunterteam[.]com</li>
<li>office-services-labs[.]com</li>
<li>office-cloud-reserve[.]com</li>
<li>office-clean-index[.]com</li>
<li>office-cleaner-indexes[.]com</li>
</ol>
<p>We collected over 130 different malware samples from various sources associated with these domains. Judging by the names and content of these samples, the spring 2020 campaign targeted users in Europe and CIS countries. Group-IB experts uncovered decoy documents in Ukrainian, Belarusian, Kazakh, Russian and Greek.</p>
<p>The campaign&#8217;s first files were uploaded to public sandboxes on March 23, 2020. One of these files was named &#8220;<em>Аналіз проекту.docx</em>&#8221; (SHA1-d8826efc7c0865c873330a25d805c95c9e64ad05) and was distributed as an attachment to the email &#8220;<em>Електронна розсилка_ Змінене замовлення.eml</em>&#8221; (SHA1-7f1fdf605e00323c055341919173a7448e3641fb), which was uploaded to VirusTotal via a web interface from Ukraine.</p>
<div id="attachment_26144" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26144" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26144" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20442'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Contents of the phishing email " width="904" height="442" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-13.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26144" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26144" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-13.png" alt="Contents of the phishing email " width="904" height="442" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26144" class="wp-caption-text">Contents of the email &#8220;Електронна розсилка_ Змінене замовлення.eml&#8221;</p></div>
<h3>Infection</h3>
<p>The document&#8217;s content doesn&#8217;t spark much interest and looks like a scanned invoice. However, the document exploits the CVE-2017-0199 vulnerability, which executes a command that loads the payload <em>http://office-cloud-reserve[.]com/hydro.exe</em>.</p>
<div id="attachment_26145" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26145" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26145" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%2090'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Executable kremlin RATs PowerShell script" width="904" height="90" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-12.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26145" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26145" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-12.png" alt="Executable kremlin RATs PowerShell script" width="904" height="90" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26145" class="wp-caption-text">Executable PowerShell script</p></div>
<p>The payload is the AgentTesla spyware . A legitimate compromised domain (<em>ftp.centredebeautenellycettier[.]fr</em>) was used as a server for data exfiltration.</p>
<div id="attachment_26146" style="width: 1620px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26146" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26146" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201610%20875'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Setting up an FTP connection" width="1610" height="875" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min-8.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26146" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26146" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min-8.png" alt="Setting up an FTP connection" width="1610" height="875" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26146" class="wp-caption-text">Setting up an FTP connection</p></div>
<p>Another analyzed file (SHA1- 19324fc16f99a92e737660c4737a41df044ecc54) called &#8220;<em>Байланысорталықтары.img</em>&#8221; was distributed as an attachment to COVID-19-themed emails (SHA1: 403c0f9a210f917e88d20d97392d9b1b14cbe310) in Kazakh.</p>
<div id="attachment_26147" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26147" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26147" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20462'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Contents of the phishing email" width="904" height="462" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-9.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26147" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26147" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-9.png" alt="Contents of the phishing email" width="904" height="462" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26147" class="wp-caption-text">Contents of the email 403c0f9a210f917e88d20d97392d9b1b14cbe310</p></div>
<p>This attachment is an .iso image. In some cases, it is named &#8220;<em>Байланыс орталықтары.img</em>&#8220;. The file is mounted to the system as an image which contains one obfuscated VBS file (SHA1: fd274f57e59c8ae3e69e0a4eb59a06ee8fd74f91) named &#8220;<em>Денсаулық сақтау бойынша анықтамалық жәнедеректер базасы.vbs</em>&#8220;. The file is a loader that executes an obfuscated PS code. After that, the file <em>http://office-cleaner-indexes[.]com/loud.jpg</em> is read.</p>
<div id="attachment_26148" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26148" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26148" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20414'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Contents of the dropped file - kremlin RATs" width="904" height="414" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-9.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26148" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26148" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-9.png" alt="Contents of the dropped file - kremlin RATs" width="904" height="414" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26148" class="wp-caption-text">Contents of the dropped file SHA1:fd274f57e59c8ae3e69e0a4eb59a06ee8fd74f91</p></div>
<p>As a result, AgentTesla is loaded and executed, which also exfiltrates the data through <em>ftp.centredebeautenellycettier[.]fr</em></p>
<p>Another document (SHA1: c992e0a46185bf0b089b3c4261e4faff15a5bc15) named &#8220;<em>Συμφωνία 060520.xls</em>&#8221; was distributed via email in Greek. Its content looks the same as all others in this campaign, but in Greek. Its NanoCore Rat payload connects to <em>screw-malwrhunterteams[.]com</em>.</p>
<div id="attachment_26149" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26149" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26149" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20244'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Contents of the greek decoy document kremlin RATs" width="904" height="244" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/20-min-8.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26149" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26149" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/20-min-8.png" alt="Contents of the greek decoy document kremlin RATs" width="904" height="244" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26149" class="wp-caption-text">Contents of the decoy document &#8220;Συμφωνία 060520.xls&#8221;</p></div>
<h2>2019 campaign</h2>
<p>Further analysis of the infrastructure related to <em>tetragulf@yahoo[.]com</em> revealed that in 2019 only four domains were registered to this email address, two of which were registered in late February and were involved in one campaign distributing malicious documents.</p>
<p>List of registered domains (those confirmed as malicious are underlined):</p>
<ul>
<li>east-ge[.]com</li>
<li>mariotkitchens[.]com</li>
<li>sommernph[.]com</li>
<li>kingtexs-tvv[.]com</li>
</ul>
<p>The first files associated with these domains were first uploaded to public sandboxes on June 18, 2019.</p>
<div id="attachment_26122" style="width: 984px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26122" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26122" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20974%20538'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="List of malicious files associated with the kremlin RATs 2019 campaign" width="974" height="538" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-7.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26122" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26122" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-7.png" alt="List of malicious files associated with the kremlin RATs 2019 campaign" width="974" height="538" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26122" class="wp-caption-text">List of malicious files associated with the 2019 campaign according to Group-IB network graph</p></div>
<p>Most of these files are RTF documents that exploit the CVE-2017-11882 vulnerability, while others are the executable payload. <strong>While investigating this campaign, we found emails and decoys in Ukrainian, Russian, Greek, Spanish, and Czech.</strong></p>
<h3>Infection</h3>
<p>One of the first documents in this campaign was distributed via email under various names: &#8220;<em>CNC 0247.doc</em>&#8220;, &#8220;<em>ЧПУ 0247.doc</em>&#8221; (SHA1: 443c079b24d65d7fd74392b90c0eac4aab67060c).</p>
<div id="attachment_26123" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26123" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26123" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20672'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Contents of the phishing email" width="904" height="672" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/22-min-6.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26123" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26123" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/22-min-6.png" alt="Contents of the phishing email" width="904" height="672" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26123" class="wp-caption-text">Contents of the email (SHA1: b6ff3e87ab7d6bd8c7abd3ee30af24b4e3709601)</p></div>
<p>According to our graph, this document connects to <em>http://68.235.38[.]157/ava.hta and kingtexs-tvv[.]com</em></p>
<div id="attachment_26124" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26124" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26124" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20496'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Network communication of the phishing file" width="904" height="496" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/23-min-6.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26124" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26124" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/23-min-6.png" alt="Network communication of the phishing file" width="904" height="496" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26124" class="wp-caption-text">Network communication of the file SHA1: 443c079b24d65d7fd74392b90c0eac4aab67060c (according to Group-IB&#8217;s network graph data)</p></div>
<p>We found this host interesting and uncovered additional files that established network connections to <em>http://68.235.38[.]157</em>. Some of these files, &#8220;<em>Estos son los documentos adjuntos de junio.doc</em>&#8221; (SHA1: 02799b41c97b6205f1999a72cef8b8991d4b8092) and &#8220;<em>New Order.doc</em>&#8221; (SHA1: 25abf0f75c56516134436c1f836d9db1e770ff30), exploit vulnerability CVE-2017-11882. At startup, they establish a connection to <em>http://68.235.38[.]157/oyii.hta</em>.</p>
<p><div id="attachment_26125" style="width: 1365px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26125" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26125" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201355%20964'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Contents of http://68.235.38[.]157/oyii.hta" width="1355" height="964" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/24-min-6.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26125" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26125" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/24-min-6.png" alt="Contents of http://68.235.38[.]157/oyii.hta" width="1355" height="964" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26125" class="wp-caption-text">Contents of http://68.235.38[.]157/oyii.hta</p></div>This file contains a Visual Basic code, which performs a Base64-encoded PowerShell command to download payload from the public file storage system <em>https://m.put[.]re/Qm8He5E4.exe</em> (SHA1: 523c5e0a1c9bc6d28f08500e96319571b57e4ba7) and stores it to the temp directory under the name &#8220;<em>avantfirewall.exe</em>&#8220;.</p>
<div id="attachment_26126" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26126" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26126" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%2054'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Executable PowerShell script" width="904" height="54" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/25-min-4.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26126" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26126" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/25-min-4.png" alt="Executable PowerShell script" width="904" height="54" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26126" class="wp-caption-text">Executable PowerShell script</p></div>
<p>The payload reads the content from <em>https://paste[.]ee/r/rSrae</em>, which results in the Async RAT being executed. The tool connects to the C&amp;C server <em>kizzoyi.duckdns[.]org</em> on port 8808. Another document from this campaign is named &#8220;<em>таблиці.doc</em>&#8221; (SHA1-1230acfd1f6f5b13a218ff8658a835997d1f0774). It was distributed via emails in Ukrainian.</p>
<p><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26127" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20628'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="screenshot of the kremlin RATs phishing email in ukranian" width="904" height="628" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/26-min-5.png" /><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26127" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/26-min-5.png" alt="screenshot of the kremlin RATs phishing email in ukranian" width="904" height="628" /></noscript></p>
<p>Once the document is run, the critical vulnerability CVE-2017-11882 allows malicious code contained in the wd32PrvSE.wmf OLE object to be executed without any user interaction.</p>
<div id="attachment_26128" style="width: 914px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26128" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26128" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20904%20482'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="OLE objects contained in phishing file" width="904" height="482" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/27-min-4.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26128" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26128" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/27-min-4.png" alt="OLE objects contained in phishing file" width="904" height="482" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26128" class="wp-caption-text">OLE objects contained in SHA1:1230acfd1f6f5b13a218ff8658a835997d1f0774</p></div>
<p>As a result of code execution from OLE objects, the Async RAT is loaded and executed.</p>
<h2>Recommendations</h2>
<p>Below you will find adversary techniques and defensive measures mapped against MITRE ATT&amp;CK and MITRE Shield, which we recommend using to prevent similar incidents.</p>
<p>All mitigation techniques are implemented in Group-IB products to ensure that our clients are protected at all attack stages. If you have any questions or suspicions about an emerging incident, <a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#b7c5d2c4c7d8d9c4d2f7d4d2c5c39ad0ded599d4d8da">please email us</a>.</p>
<p><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26129" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201500%20843'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Kremlin RATs campaigns MITRE ATT&amp;CK and MITRE Shield" width="1500" height="843" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/28-min.webp" /><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26129" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/28-min.webp" alt="Kremlin RATs campaigns MITRE ATT&amp;CK and MITRE Shield" width="1500" height="843" /></noscript></p>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    const postcontent = document.getElementById('blog-post-content');
    const h2counter = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2');

    for (i = 0; i < h2counter.length; i++) {
        const h2elementtag = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i];
        const h2element = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i].innerHTML;
        let postnavitem = document.createElement('a');
        postnavitem.classList.add('post-navigation__item', 'subtitle2', 'scrollto');
        postnavitem.innerHTML = h2element;
        const postnavlink = postnavitem.innerHTML;
        let str = postnavlink;
        str = str.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\u0400-\u04FF]/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/\s+/g, '-').toLowerCase();
        str = str.replace(/^-+|-+$/g, '');
        postnavitem.classList += ' ' + str;
        postnavitem.href = '#' + str;
        h2elementtag.id = str;
        document.getElementById('post-navigation__wrapper').appendChild(postnavitem);
    }

    const sections = document.querySelectorAll('h2');
    const navItems = document.querySelectorAll('.post-navigation__item');

    window.addEventListener('scroll', () => {
        let current = '';
        sections.forEach((section) => {
            const sectionTop = section.offsetTop;
            const sectionHeight = section.clientHeight;
            if (pageYOffset >= sectionTop - sectionHeight / 10) {
                current = section.getAttribute('id');
            }
        });

        navItems.forEach((a) => {
            a.classList.remove('active');
            if (a.classList.contains(current)) {
                a.classList.add('active');
            }
        });
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrollto').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300,
        );

        return false;
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL WITH NO HASH
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrolltonohash').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300
        );

        return false;
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    function readingTime() {
        const text = document.getElementById("blog-post-content").innerText;
        const wpm = 225;
        const words = text.trim().split(/\s+/).length;
        const time = Math.ceil(words / wpm);
        document.getElementById("reading-time").innerText = time;
    }
    readingTime();
</script>

    function positioningSidebar() {
        let sidebar = document.getElementById('sidebar');
        let sidebarHeight = sidebar.offsetHeight;
        let windowHeight = document.documentElement.clientHeight;
        if (windowHeight < (sidebarHeight + 48)) {
            let sidebarAuthors = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__authors');
            let sidebarMorePosts = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__more-posts');
            let authorsHeight = sidebarAuthors ? sidebarAuthors.offsetHeight : 0;
            let morePostsHeight = sidebarMorePosts ? sidebarMorePosts.offsetHeight : 0;
            let sidebarTop = authorsHeight + morePostsHeight + 140;
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', -sidebarTop + 'px');
        } else {
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', '72px');
        }
    }
    window.addEventListener('resize', positioningSidebar);
    positioningSidebar();

    if (localStorage.getItem('theme')) {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
        } else if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'light') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--white');
        }
    } else {
        localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
    }

    jQuery('.theme-toggle').click(function() {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'light');
        } else {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        }
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--dark');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--white');
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        }, 250);
    });
    jQuery('.share-toggle').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-toggle').toggleClass('active');
        jQuery('.share-widget').toggleClass('show');
    });
</script>
